Do not filter, skip, or reinterpret.\nset -eu\n# The version setup_harness.sh installs. run_conformance.sh refuses a different one, so a\n# passing verdict names a specific exam rather than whichever harness was on PATH.\nTOML_TEST_VERSION=\"${TOML_TEST_VERSION:-v2.2.0}\"\nexport TOML_TEST_VERSION\ngo build -o toml-decoder ./cmd/toml-decoder\nDECODER=\"$PWD/toml-decoder\" exec sh sources/run_conformance.sh\n=== sources/run_conformance.sh\n#!/bin/sh\n# Run the upstream toml-test conformance suite against the built decoder.\n#\n# Imported sources land in sources/ inside the application directory, so this is\n# normally invoked as `sh sources/run_conformance.sh` from that directory.\n#\n# Usage:\n#   DECODER=./toml-decoder sh sources/run_conformance.sh                        # full suite\n#   DECODER=./toml-decoder sh sources/run_conformance.sh -run 'valid/string/*'  # one group\n#   DECODER=./toml-decoder sh sources/run_conformance.sh -json                  # machine-readable\n#\n# Environment:\n#   DECODER    decoder command. Required — this harness is language-neutral and\n#              deliberately has no default implementation language.\n#   TOML_TEST  absolute path to the toml-test binary (default: found on PATH)\n#\n# Exit code follows the harness exit-status standard in prompts/UAT_SETUP_INSTRUCTIONS.md:\n# 0 only when every test passes, 2 when this harness could not run at all, and any other nonzero\n# when it ran and the decoder failed. A precondition of the harness is never charged to the build.\n\nset -u\n\nif [ -z \"${DECODER:-}\" ]; then\n    echo \"error: DECODER is not set; give the command that runs your decoder.\" >&2\n    exit 2\nfi\n\nif [ -n \"${TOML_TEST:-}\" ] && [ -x \"${TOML_TEST}\" ]; then\n    HARNESS=\"${TOML_TEST}\"\nelif command -v toml-test >/dev/null 2>&1; then\n    HARNESS=\"$(command -v toml-test)\"\nelse\n    cat >&2 <<'EOF'\nerror: toml-test not found.\n\nInstall the harness once:\n\n    sh sources/setup_harness.sh\n\nor set TOML_TEST to an existing binary:\n\n    TOML_TEST=/path/to/toml-test sh sources/run_conformance.sh\nEOF\n    # 2, not the shell's own 127: an uninstalled harness says nothing about the decoder, and 127\n    # would be read as an ordinary nonzero exit and charged against the build as a product FAIL.\n    exit 2\nfi\n\n# The suite's identity is part of the verdict. Without this, \"the authoritative suite passed\"\n# names no particular suite: whichever toml-test happened to be on PATH decided the run, and a\n# different version on a different machine is a different exam. Callers inspect\n// it with errors.Is; the wrapped message names the line that failed.\nvar ErrSyntax = errors.New(\"invalid TOML\")\n\n// Decode reads a TOML document from r and returns its tagged-value model.\n// Each leaf maps to {\"type\", \"value\"} with the value as a string; each table maps to\n// an object, and each array of tables maps to an array of objects.\n// Values are limited to strings, booleans, integers, and floats so far.\nfunc Decode(r io.Reader) (map[string]any, error) {\n\tdata, err := io.ReadAll(r)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"read document: %w\", err)\n\t}\n\tif !utf8.Valid(data) {\n\t\treturn nil, fmt.Errorf(\"%w: document is not valid UTF-8\", ErrSyntax)\n\t}\n\tp := &parser{src: string(data)}\n\tdoc, err := p.document()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\treturn doc.export(), nil\n}\n\n// parser scans src by byte offset. Multi-byte runes are copied through unchanged,\n// so the ASCII delimiters the scanner checks are never confused with them.\ntype parser struct {\n\tsrc string\n\tpos int\n}\n\n// document reads every line into the root table. cur is the table that key/value pairs\n// land in; it changes at each header.\nfunc (p *parser) document() (*table, error) {\n\troot := newTable(originHeader)\n\tcur := root\n\tfor {\n\t\tp.skipSpaces()\n\t\tif p.done() {\n\t\t\treturn root, nil\n\t\t}\n\t\tswitch c := p.src[p.pos]; {\n\t\tcase c == '#' || c == '\\n' || c == '\\r':\n\t\t\tif err := p.endOfLine(); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\tcase c == '[':\n\t\t\tparts, array, err := p.header()\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif cur, err = p.enterHeader(root, parts, array); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\tdefault:\n\t\t\tparts, value, err := p.keyValue()\n\t\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tif err := p.insert(cur, parts, value); err != nil {\n\t\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err := p.endOfLine(); err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn nil, err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (p *parser) keyValue() ([]string, any, error) {\n\tparts, err := p.key()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t}\n\tname := strings.Join(parts, \".\")\n\tp.skipSpaces()\n\tif p.done() || p.src[p.pos] != '=' {\n\t\treturn nil, nil, p.errorf(\"expected '=' after key %q\", name)\n\t}\n\tp.pos++\n\tp.skipSpaces()\n\tvalue, err := p.value()\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, nil, err\n\t}\n\treturn parts, value, nil\n}\n\n// value reads one value. Strings start with a quote and arrays and inline tables with a\n// bracket or brace; everything else is a bare boolean, integer, or float. The result is\n// a tagged model, an arrayValue, or a closed *table. The longer string delimiters are\n// tested first so that `\"\"\"` is never read as `\"\"`.\nfunc (p *parser) value() (any, error) {\n\tif p.done() {\n\t\treturn nil, p.errorf(\"expected a value\")\n\t}\n\tvar s string\n\tvar err error\n\tswitch {\n\tcase strings.HasPrefix(p.src[p.pos:], `\"\"\"`):\n\t\ts, err = p.multiline('\"', true)\n\tcase p.src[p.pos] == '\"':\n\t\ts, err = p.basicString()\n\tcase strings.HasPrefix(p.src[p.pos:], \"'''\"):\n\t\ts, err = p.multiline('\\'', false)\n\tcase p.src[p.pos] == '\\'':\n\t\ts, err = p.literalString()\n\tcase p.src[p.pos] == '[':\n\t\treturn p.array()\n\tcase p.src[p.pos] == '{':\n\t\treturn p.inlineTable()\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn p.scalar()\n\t}\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn nil, err\n\t}\n\treturn map[string]any{\"type\": \"string\", \"value\": s}, nil\n}\n\n// endOfLine consumes trailing spaces, an optional comment, and the line break.\n// End of input also ends a line.\nfunc (p *parser) endOfLine() error {\n\tp.skipSpaces()\n\tif !p.done() && p.src[p.pos] == '#' {\n\t\tp.pos++\n\t\tfor !p.done() && !p.atNewline() {\n\t\t\tif isDisallowedControl(p.src[p.pos]) {\n\t\t\t\treturn p.errorf(\"control character in comment\")\n\t\t\t}\n\t\t\tp.pos++\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif p.done() {\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tif p.src[p.pos] == '\\n' {\n\t\tp.pos++\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\tif p.atCRLF() {\n\t\tp.pos += 2\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\treturn p.errorf(\"unexpected text after value\")\n}\n\nfunc (p *parser) skipSpaces() {\n\tfor !p.done() && (p.src[p.pos] == ' ' || p.src[p.pos] == '\\t') {\n\t\tp.pos++\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (p *parser) done() bool {\n\treturn p.pos >= len(p.src)\n}\n\nfunc (p *parser) atCRLF() bool {\n\treturn strings.HasPrefix(p.src[p.pos:], \"\\r\\n\")\n}\n\nfunc (p *parser) atNewline() bool {\n\treturn !p.done() && (p.src[p.pos] == '\\n' || p.atCRLF())\n}\n\nfunc (p *parser) errorf(format string, args ...any) error {\n\tline := 1 + strings.Count(p.src[:p.pos], \"\\n\")\n\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"%w: line %d: %s\", ErrSyntax, line, fmt.Sprintf(format, args...))\n}\n\nfunc isBareKeyByte(c byte) bool {\n\treturn (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || c == '-' || c == '_'\n}\n\n// isDisallowedControl reports whether c is a control character TOML forbids in\n// strings and comments. Tab is the only control character it allows. Line breaks\n// are excluded here because each caller decides whether a line break is legal.\nfunc isDisallowedControl(c byte) bool {\n\treturn (c < 0x20 && c != '\\t' && c != '\\n') || c == 0x7f\n}\n=== toml_test.go\npackage toml\n\nimport (\n\t\"errors\"\n\t\"reflect\"\n\t\"strings\"\n\t\"testing\"\n)\n\nfunc decodeString(input string) (map[string]any, error) {\n\treturn
